Three things generalize. **A capability the runtime does not state is a capability the environment states**, and an unversioned per-machine profile is not a place a §1.2 invariant may live; if a flag decides whether a signature is honest, the flag belongs on the argv the runtime builds. **State the allowlist rather than the deny list wherever the CLI's tool registry is enumerable and validated** — omp rejects an unknown name in `--tools`, so an allowlist makes a typo loud and makes every future tool absent by default, whereas a deny list re-grants each new one silently; Claude takes the deny list only because its built-in set is not enumerable from here with the same confidence. And **the membership is measured, not imagined**: the permitted set is the union of the tools this run's 39 reviewer and 48 builder sessions actually invoked, minus the two delegation tools, because an allowlist authored from intuition contains the actor by starving it. What this does **not** do is contain a hostile actor — `bash` remains on every allowlist, so the containment is §9.6's honest limit again: it stops the reviewer that reaches for the delegation tool sitting in its schema, not one determined to route around its absence. Two incidental corrections to rows above: the lock-only count in M24's evidence cell is 7 against a corpus that has since reached 8, and the delegation was never confined to the stalled reviews. |
| M28 | **A state written by an operator escape, indistinguishable downstream from the same state reached by the machine.** `maestro skip` writes `node_lifecycle.state = 'MERGED'` and an `output_sha`, and it performs no merge. The five identity gates it runs are real and are the reason it is trustworthy — the accepted SHA must be a canonical digest, descend from the latest attempt base, be an ancestor of HEAD, *equal* HEAD, and leave a clean tree — but the row it then writes was byte-identical in shape to the row `mark_merged` writes for a node the run verified and merged. Nothing in `node_lifecycle`, in any read verb, or in the visualizer carried the difference. In git it was visible and nowhere else: a merged lane leaves a merge commit and a skipped lane leaves only the attempt commit, so reading the integration branch's log was the sole way to tell, which is the reconstruction-from-artifacts §1.2 forbids. Underneath sat the §1.1 item 4 breach: an agent node merged this way carries none of the chain that item enumerates — no post-node gate pass, no reviewer verdict, no permission check over a measured delta — and **nothing recorded that the chain was absent**, so no audit could find it afterwards. | `run-2a44d226e75a4be391a14f02b78a6d25`, node `lane-p5-gap-policy`: rendered `MERGED  attempt 3 -> df98283ee4f2` above three attempts reading `CANCELLED`, `CANCELLED`, `BLOCKED`, none carrying a verdict. Zero `SEMANTIC` transitions across six attempts — no reviewer had ever produced a verdict on it — and the skip merged 928 lines on a gate run and an operator's word. The display was the honest shape of a skip; the reasonable reading of it was that the run had merged the node and the attempt list was stale. | An operator reading `run status` after a rescue and being unable to tell which of two MERGED lanes the run had actually merged | **The precedent was already in the ledger one state along, and the fix is to copy it rather than invent.** `CANCELLED` had been one word carrying two facts until M18 split it with `cancel_cause`, a typed column stamped in the transaction that writes the state, with an unrecorded value that reads as *cannot tell* and never as the benign case. `MERGED` was the same defect, unsplit, and `abandon` was already doing the right thing beside `skip` — which is the tell worth generalizing: **when one operator verb in a family stamps a cause and its sibling does not, the sibling is the defect, not the exception.** `MergeCause` is `SCHEDULER` or `OPERATOR_ACCEPTED`, and a MERGED row written before the column reads `UNRECORDED`, because backfilling it as `SCHEDULER` would have every pre-existing row assert an evidence chain nobody checked. Two further things generalize. **The state and the provenance are two facts and must not be collapsed into one**: the state stays `MERGED` because the frontier, the readiness predicate and the run outcome all key on it and a display-only seventh state would be §7.3's vocabulary defect returning; the provenance rides beside it. And **an escape that legitimately bypasses the evidence chain must record that it did** — not refuse, since refusing strands the operator §11.3 exists for, but write down what the ledger could and could not show at the moment of the write, so the audit is a query rather than a re-derivation from git. The audit half rides the transition, as `retry --grant`'s magnitude does, while the column carries the one fact a reader keys on (§1.1 item 4, §1.2, §7.3, §11.3, issue #93). |
| M29 | **A budget with a spend counter and no boundary: `attempts_spent` counted every attempt of a class over the node's whole life, and nothing debited, expired, or reset it.** A resume was already a state boundary — `_write_resume_transition` clears `cancel_requested` there, because the operator withdrew the stop request by resuming — and the retry budgets crossed it untouched. So a node carried spend from defects that had since been fixed, forever, and the arithmetic was not recoverable by any means the design offered: the only route to more room was raising `execution:` in the deployment's `maestro.config.yaml`, a persistent edit to a deployment-owned file expressing a one-run intent, which raises the ceiling for every node in the run to make a decision about one. | `lane-p5-gap-policy` in `run-2a44d226e75a4be391a14f02b78a6d25`: three `LAUNCHER_TRANSIENT` attempts burned in **ten seconds** relaunching against a herdr workspace that had already been destroyed (M-#79), against `launcher_retries: 2`. #79 is fixed and that mode cannot recur; the three rows remained, so the node sat permanently over budget and the next launcher failure of any kind — including a genuinely transient one — would block it on first contact. The same node then spent its ENVIRONMENTAL budget on the reviewer that never reported (#89) and blocked `ENVIRONMENTAL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`; after an operator `retry` it was `PENDING` with both budgets exhausted, scheduled only to block again. | Reading the ledger of a run that had already blocked, after #79 and #90 were both fixed — the provenance §19's preamble names as costing no budget of its own. | **A budget is counted from a boundary, and the boundary is a typed transition.** `node_lifecycle.retry_spend_floor` holds the attempt number a node's retry-class spend is counted from; `attempts_spent` counts only attempts above it. It is raised in the same transaction as a resume (every node in the run) and as an operator `retry` (the one node it names, which is the granularity a run-scoped flag cannot express), and by nothing else — not by an attempt succeeding, not by time passing, not by anything an agent says, so §1.2 holds of the number a budget check reads. A **floor**, never a decrement: the attempt rows are the evidence chain, and refreshing a budget by rewriting what it counted would destroy the record of the failures that produced it. It is raised to the highest attempt *already classified*, which is what leaves the accounting around it intact — the ENVIRONMENTAL charge a resume levies on an inherited attempt, and the one an escape levies on the attempt it closes, both land above the floor and are still charged, while §9.7's exemption for an attempt that declared an accepted result still costs nothing. The class this fixes is wider than the instance: the same shape survives in the review and semantic ceilings, which are cumulative by deliberate design and rescued instead by #81's sized grant, and the two mechanisms now meet on one command — `retry --grant N` sizes the ceilings, and the same escape refreshes the retry classes. |
| M30 | **A detector wired on one path and not on the operator's, so the crudest clock was the only one left.** `FinalizationWindow` has carried an `actor_status` seam since #89, and every sub-span detector gated on it — `ACTOR_ABANDONED` for a reviewer that went live and then stopped without declaring, `NEVER_STARTED` for one that never went live at all — reads the route through it. `_reviewer_window_factory`, the window `maestro plan finalize` builds, passed no reader; `_code_review_runner`'s window, a few hundred lines below in the same file, passed one. The operator path therefore held the span bound and a raw turn clock and nothing in between, and could not separate a reviewer that never started from one that stopped without declaring from one that was working the whole time — the exact three-way distinction §3.6 B14 was written to require. The turn clock then fired unconditionally, below a B14-compliant quiescence branch that could never arm, and convicted the third case. | `maestro plan finalize cmo-consolidation-l-r5` returned `FINALIZATION_STALLED signal=TURN_TIMEOUT after 128.6s` against a reviewer whose Herdr pane was still advancing its revision counter. No receipt was written, and the plan stayed unfinalizable until `--reviewer-turn-timeout-s` was raised by hand — the wall clock B14 refused, re-entered as the operator's only remedy. | Reading the finalization trace after a reviewer that was demonstrably alive was killed. | **A lesson is extracted when it holds on every path its own incident named, not when it is implemented once.** B14's remedy — quiescence-after-liveness read through a status seam, never a wall clock — was built and wired to one window, and the window left unwired was `plan finalize`, the verb named in B14's own evidence. A seam existing is not a reader being passed: an `actor_status` keyword with a `None` default silently disables every detector behind it, and a degraded window is indistinguishable from a healthy one at the type level, so nothing downstream can report the loss. The factory now passes `read_status`, which answers raw per-pane `agent_status` and never `observe()` — that call collapses idle into RUNNING and cannot express "went live, then stopped" — the turn clock is gated on the route reporting the actor live, and `finalization_window.DEFAULT_TURN_TIMEOUT_S` is the single constant the CLI default is taken from rather than a second literal that would bind last and silently win. This is §19 M6 at a different layer: a guarantee installed on a path rather than at the point every route crosses. The run-side sibling is **not** fixed — §16.3 item 59. |
| M31 | **A review rejection discarded the diff it had rejected, so the next attempt re-implemented the node instead of repairing it.** A rejection classified SEMANTIC recycled the attempt, and the next attempt branched from the integration head like any other — from an empty file, carrying the reviewer's findings across as prompt text and nothing else. Consecutive attempts were therefore independent implementations, each judged fresh, rather than iterations of one artifact; findings could not descend because nothing accumulated, and a nearly accepted tree was deleted rather than corrected. | `lane-p5-gap-policy` of `run-fb9973646d344400a9e4f4d7818d00f2` was rejected four times and produced 2, 2, 1, and 3 findings across those attempts, with `base_sha` identical — `586a5d2ed7` — on every attempt row. Attempt 3 reached a single finding and that work was deleted. The predecessor run `run-2a44d226` burned 23 attempts the same way. A `review_ceiling` of 6 therefore bought six one-shot guesses, not six rounds of refinement. | The ledger of a lane that exhausted its review budget, read back attempt by attempt | A retry is an iteration only if it starts from what the previous attempt produced. Where the failure is a *judgement about an artifact* rather than a failure to produce one, discarding the artifact discards the only thing the judgement was about, and a budget that reads as N chances to converge is N independent first attempts. The signature is checkable without reading a line of prose: consecutive attempts sharing a `base_sha` across a rejection. The repair is admitted by structural refusals over typed rows — the previous attempt's classification, the attempt's own published output commit, an unmoved integration head, a chain under `REPAIR_CHAIN_LIMIT`, and a finding count that did not rise — never by the reviewer's prose (§1.2); every refusal falls back to the fresh base the scheduler used before, so nothing here can block a node. This is #90's repair applied to the other actor: there a stalled reviewer was re-dispatched against the attempt's surviving output commit, here a builder whose diff was rejected is sent back to that same commit. |
| M32 | **A report that collapsed "cannot be said" onto "did not happen", and asserted the negative about a run that was still executing.** `run convergence` partitioned the derived run state into finished and not finished with no third answer, so a run between polls — BLOCKED or PENDING on the node rows, with a live scheduler behind it — read as one that had already ended. Whether a scheduler process is still behind the run is not in the node rows at all, so no partition of them could have answered it. | The verdict line printed "not converged — run ended first" directly beside the live attempt rows the same report had just rendered. | An operator reading a convergence report during a live run, against the data printed beneath it | A verdict about whether something has stopped is not derivable from the state rows alone, and a reader forced to partition a nine-member enum will pick a side rather than decline. Give the reader the tri-state instead: `run_in_flight` answers `True`, `False`, or `None`, composing the derived state with scheduler liveness the way `run status` already reports the two side by side, and `None` renders as unknown rather than as either boolean. "Not converged" is a verdict; on a run that has not finished the honest text is "not converged yet", cause `run still in flight`. A report that states a terminal fact about a live system is worse than one that declines to state it, because it is read as evidence. |

### 19.4 Incidents of 2026-08-20

| ID | Failure | Evidence | Found by | Lesson that binds future work |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M33 | **Three of four adjudication layers answered in prose, and all three failed on the same plan; the one that counted was correct.** On plan digest `aa73fb53` (`cmo-consolidation-l-r6`, lane `lane-p5-gap-policy`) plan review returned PASS with 0 findings; plan finalization cleared 57 of 58 matrix cells with six template sentences and its only finding was the canary planted to detect a reviewer that had not read the cell — a reviewer whose prompt was the matrix, the digest and a report path, never the plan; node code review rejected the lane 12 times over 39 attempts across four `run_id`s and never converged, because `review_ceiling` was counted per run and every new run re-armed the budget the last had exhausted; final acceptance — ancestry sweep, integration gate, `adjudicate_gate(gate, min_cases)` — counted and was right. The requirement itself (`req-p5-gap-policy`) asserted a per-source cursor that persists while declaring a gate that runs entirely against an in-memory SQLite session, so no attempt could produce the evidence the reviewer was asked to judge, and no typed field said so. | `lexgenius-pipeline/.maestro/plans/cmo-consolidation-l-r6.plan.json` and its shipped bytes (sha256 `aa73fb53…`); the ledger's four runs for that digest: 39 attempts, 12 `review_rejected` rows on one node; the finalization receipt's 58 cells and its canary finding | An operator reading the ledger after the fourth run | **A lifecycle transition may depend only on a counted, re-derivable fact, never on a model's prose — drawn once, for the whole factory (§1.2).** Three consequences, each landed the same day: a layer that cannot state its verdict as a count is not an adjudication layer — plan finalization is removed, two of its eleven checks promoted into `plan_validate` as `GATE_SELECTOR_NODE_SCOPED` and `INTEGRATION_GATE_COVERS_LANES`, nine deleted, none migrated to another reviewer (§6.5); a prose layer that survives must terminate on a count — node review stays (B12) but its budget is `review_attempts_across_runs`, and `run start` refuses `NODE_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED_ACROSS_RUNS` (§7.5); a claim no fixture can witness is refused before it ships — `claims[].witness {scope, store}` is a required typed field and `CLAIM_UNWITNESSABLE` refuses a `cross_invocation` claim over a store that dies with the invocation, reading no prose (§6.4). The general form: when a layer fails, ask first whether its verdict was ever a count; if it was not, the fix is not a better prompt, a smarter canary, or a second reviewer — it is to remove the layer or bound it by a number the ledger already holds. |
